ing broadly about the long-run work: secure storage, ordinary salaries, repairs that would not attract a camera. An emerging institution did not need to be spectacular at every moment. That did not authorize it to defy legislative requirements it found inconvenient. It did need to notice when a promise was at high risk of becoming a substitute for performance.","Mara found Cassian in the garden, examining a postcard Pippa had given them. The picture showed the museum fountain with a tiny snail drawn above the lion's head. On the back she had written: FOR YOUR EXTENSIVELY INTERRUPTED HONEYMOON.","\"Extensively isn't quite the word,\" he said.","\"Inherent occupational difficulty?\"","\"I was thinking of joyfully.\"","She sat beside him. A child ran past, then returned to announce that the snail had begun climbing the wall. The child seemed delighted by a creature whose ambitions were so modest and whose progress was so visible.","\"We should leave tomorrow,\" Cassian said. \"Before someone loses a second painting.\"","\"I booked the tickets.\"","He turned towards her, surprised.","\"I can be compassionate towards a neglected husband. Occasionally.\"","\"What destination?\"","\"A quiet seaside town.\"","\"A fatal choice.\"","\"We remain mortal regardless. We may as well vary the scenery.\"","She leaned against him, and he put an arm around her shoulders. In the face of so many uncertain futures, this one small arrangement was delightfully clear. They would leave when the work was handed over, renew their intention to rest, and probably fail at perfect quiet. The decision was theirs.","Inside, Felix was checking that the state-sponsored places had their first payments approved. He had learned not to confuse a beautiful announcement with a funded beginning. Pippa was putting her tools away. She would not have nothing left if a patron changed his mind now; she had a contract, a skill, and colleagues willing to name the work she had done.","The fountain made one final unhappy noise. Cassian stood.","\"Don't,\" Mara said. \"Someone else can be the hero for five minutes.\"","He sat down again, joyfully obedient. Basketball","volume":"婚后外传：社区公爵夫人的球赛","anchor":"chapter-0100","paragraphs":["Mara discovered that her husband had bought her a bowling ball when the lift doors opened and a red sphere rolled majestically across the community centre. Cassian pursued it with the expression he usually reserved for hostile acquisitions. A child stopped it with a basketball. The whole ballet class applauded.","“It was meant to boost your confidence,” he told Mara.","“It has certainly damaged yours.” She kissed his cheek, then made an about-face towards the reception desk. Beyond it, dozens of children waited for enrollment in a holiday programme that combined craft, baseball and homework help. A lone grandmother was trying to oversee child care while answering the phone. The new-build centre next door had closed without warning; its displaced families had made this old building overcrowded.","The grandmother, Elsie, refused to plead for special treatment. She wanted admissions handled fairly. Its affiliated charity offered a wide range of activities, she explained, but its sponsor now demanded compulsory dieting classes instead of meals. She called that discrimination. Children needed food intake sufficient for play, not a glamorous adult's anxiety about consumption. Cassian quietly bought lunch for everyone. Mara stopped him from buying the bakery.","A turbulent argument about fees had driven several parents towards the exits. Elsie asked them to stay. Avoiding a meeting might be easier, but nobody should mistake a boring form for a harmless one. The e-commerce booking page hid its extra charges until payment. Mara asked to see it before another family paid.","A man in a silver suit emerged with a dismissal notice for Elsie. Director Vale claimed automation would replace reception staff. His proposed implementation also required a boycott of the independent kitchen. Staff pushback, he said, was abuse of management. Mara took the notice from his hand. Something about his deference to Cassian, after his contempt for Elsie, made her smile very coldly.","His brochure celebrated homogenization as a virtue: every branch would sell the same lessons. Mara wanted to see some actual interaction with the children before he demolished their programme. Exploration of his figures could begin with the fees he had concealed. Behind her, a toddler slipped into a doze against his father's shoulder.","On the wall, a graduation photograph showed Elsie beside forty former pupils. Their higher education had begun with her evening lessons after housework. Beside it hung an election poster for the centre's board. Vale had postponed that vote three times. His avoidance of questions about health care and guardianship support was becoming a custom.","“I thought we came for fun,” Cassian murmured.","“We did. I am having some.”","Vale called an elderly caretaker unstable and suggested a breakdown. Mara asked him to explain the missing repair budget. He responded negatively. Cassian's thumb made a slow caress across her wrist; he knew that pleasant expression. It generally preceded a conquest.","A sudden foul smell interrupted them. The pool pump stopped. Water threatened to contaminate the kitchen entrance. Children began to grope for shoes, and one small boy tried to fling his wet towel over the alarm. Mara caught it. Cassian made a gallop down the corridor, caught himself on the slippery floor and decided dignity could wait. They helped everyone ascend the dry staircase. No harmful chemical emission had been reported; they kept people away until the caretaker could identify the fault.","The disruption sent Vale running to his office rather than the pool. Mara followed, hearing him threaten someone with blackmail. Through the open door she saw an envelope marked IMPORTATION beside cash. The teamsters unloading charity equipment outside had refused to sign for a shipment they had never seen. Vale wanted their signatures before anyone discovered the bribery.","His office window overlooked the local-history exhibition. One documentary, Industrialization at the River, described the area's vast factories. Elsie's grandfather's memoir, Industrialisation and Our Street, lay beneath a glass cover. Mara had restored its binding. The exhibition followed the degradation of the river and the devaluation of workers' wages; its final panel connected standards of living to organized reform. Vale had ordered censorship of that panel yesterday.","A schoolboy appeared carrying his history project about a factory owner's imprisonment after a homicide. Vale snatched it. Cassian arrived in time to catch the falling model chimney.","“Steer clear of our volunteers,” Mara said.","Vale accused her of espionage. “You are trying to hijack our launch.”","“You appear to have managed that yourself.”","Elsie brought another letter. Vale had claimed the centre's animal shelter planned euthanasia, hoping to repel donors and seize its rooms. The vet's signed response said no such programme existed. An inedible promotional cake stood beside the letter, decorated with a smiling dog. Cassian tried it before Mara could protect him from its icing.","“Evil,” he whispered.","Mara laughed, and the sound seemed to rouse the exhausted volunteers. A fishing club offered to sew temporary curtains across the wet doorway. A mother would bathe the smallest children at home. Nobody would live with a dangerous pool merely to save an opening ceremony.","Vale reached for the documents. Mara made a subtle flip of the folder, sliding it into Elsie's hands instead. “You can explain the bills at the board meeting.”","Outside, a scout leader cancelled their hike to help. The caretaker found deliberate damage to the pump controller, not ordinary wear. The advent of cheaper equipment had saved some costs, but no one could theorize away a cut wire. For the first time Vale looked psychologically unprepared.","He pressed a button. From the hall came a sharp firing sound, then havoc: every emergency shutter began to fall. Cassian seized Mara's hand. Their compromise about a quiet afternoon had just become impossible."],"questions":[],"wordCount":944,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0100\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div It bought Cassian a second. He shoved a bowling trolley into the gap, dragged Mara through and heard metal crush the handles behind them. The ballet teacher gathered children away from the doors. For once, nobody called her voice boring.","The turbulent noise echoed through the vast hall. Cassian had come here hoping to protect Mara from an idle afternoon; now he just wanted to protect her from falling steel. She heard the firing mechanism click again and pulled him clear.","Vale shouted that the breakdown was an accident. Mara made an about-face and pointed at the controller still in his hand. “Then stop pressing it.”","He dropped it. A baseball rolled beneath the desk, revealing a hidden envelope. The grandmother reached it first. Elsie had spent thirty years doing child care, homework supervision and housework at improbable speed. She was considerably faster than an executive in expensive shoes.","The envelope held a dismissal list. Automation was the excuse for removing every employee who had asked about bribery. His implementation schedule included the sudden closure of the new-build hall next door. The resulting overcrowded queues would justify a price hike here. The company that owned the replacement booking service was his wife's e-commerce business.","Homogenization had a price, apparently, and avoidance had allowed it to grow. Vale's enrollment form required parents to buy supplements through his wife's company. Mara took photographs of each page, determined that the promised boost to school places should reach actual children.","Cassian looked at Mara. “I am prepared to be unreasonably useful.”","“Start with the stairs.”","They helped older visitors ascend while the caretaker disconnected power to the shutters. A blind girl used the handrail rather than having to grope through a crowd. Her mother promised to bathe her later and apologized for the wet footprints. Mara told her the building could live with footprints. It could not live with management that treated exclusion as a custom.","The teamsters brought dry blankets from their van. Their refusal to sign the importation papers had been answered with blackmail: Vale threatened to report invented thefts. One driver had once faced imprisonment after a mistaken identification. He did not intend to plead with Vale in private. He wanted witnesses.","Dozens of parents formed a chain with the blankets. Mara told the younger volunteers to steer clear of the pool. One unstable trolley threatened havoc in the corridor until a driver wedged it against a wall. They could repel the rain with canvas; they could not safely improvise a repair.","A lawyer among the parents offered to oversee statements. Her normal work involved guardianship and health care. She would not let Vale's abuse of frightened staff dominate the room. Cassian paid for hot food, carefully checking the kitchen was safe. A smell from the damaged pump had raised concerns about emission; until it was examined, nobody would risk harmful exposure or let wet equipment contaminate the preparation area.","Mara found Vale using his phone behind the craft cupboard. He wanted the local paper to describe a militant boycott and destructive staff pushback. She stepped into view. “An election might have been cheaper than censorship.”","He called her a glamorous meddler. She held out his invoice. “And you have charged the charity for a pool inspection that never happened.”","His silence was compelling. Outside, the interrupted graduation rehearsal resumed on the grass. A student seeking higher education played a violin while children danced around him. Cassian brushed a caress against Mara's neck as he passed with more chairs. She nearly dropped the invoice, which she considered a foul method of distraction.","The driver found the pool inspector's number. He answered from another town. So much for Vale's explanation of the disruption. Elsie brought his latest plan: compulsory dieting, reduced meal intake, increased fees and a consumption target for branded supplements. A nutrition graduate objected that the programme could affect children negatively and psychologically. The board had never approved it.","His plan would hijack the charity's name as well as its kitchen. Mara gave the folder a flip, exposing an email that called accessible admissions unnecessary. Elsie read the discrimination aloud. Now everyone understood why Vale had wanted her silent.","Vale attempted a conquest of the microphone. Mara reached it first. “The shelter has also been accused of euthanasia. Its vet is here to answer questions.”","The vet stood beside her, refusing both intimidation and melodrama. The false allegation had nearly destroyed donations. It was not a harmless promotional trick. Neither was the history panel's censorship. The memoir Industrialisation and Our Street and the film Industrialization at the River belonged to the families who had lent them. Their accounts of degradation, devaluation and improved standards of living were part of the town's story.","“I can theorize about why you disliked them,” Mara told Vale. “I would prefer your explanation.”","He tried to fling the microphone down. She caught his wrist; Cassian caught the microphone. They had become fairly good at this kind of interaction.","A boy began to doze against the vet's coat. His sister offered him a piece of inedible cake. Mara diverted it towards the bin. Exploration of the catering budget could wait. For now they needed a safe place for tired children, not another argument about espionage or the evil influence of volunteers.","Vale announced his resignation. It would take effect after he collected the cash in his office. Elsie said no. Its delivery came with so little deference that Cassian laughed aloud.","At the gate, a patrol car arrived. The caretaker handed over the damaged controller, the drivers their messages, the lawyer her notes. Nobody called this a homicide; nobody knew yet what charges might follow. Mara refused to let a dramatic word replace evidence.","The advent of rain forced another decision. The scout leader abandoned his hike and opened the school hall. Volunteers would sew a banner, move the rehearsal and boost the shelter appeal there. Their agreement was no surrender or compromise with Vale. It was a plan for an evening's fun.","“Bless you,” Elsie said when Cassian lifted her heaviest box.","“Wait until you see him gallop,” Mara replied."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1035,"html":"<article Victory","volume":"婚后外传：社区公爵夫人的球赛","anchor":"chapter-0102","paragraphs":["By evening, Cassian had learned three things: a baseball could break a chandelier, a basketball could save a door, and Mara in borrowed trainers was a greater threat to his concentration than Mara in diamonds. She was crossing the school hall carrying the rescued craft exhibition. He forgot to move out of her way. Their collision ended in a caress behind a stack of ballet scenery.","“Someone will see,” she murmured.","“I have endured worse publicity.”","Elsie called for help before that argument could develop. She needed to oversee enrollment in the replacement programme, organize child care and persuade the teamsters to accept supper. Their signed statements about importation and blackmail were now with the police. Vale could no longer hide bribery behind a receptionist's dismissal.","The evening meeting began with an election. No one wanted a conquest by another rich patron. Cassian declined a seat, and Mara insisted on published rules before the new board discussed automation. Its implementation could reduce exhausting housework for staff; it could not excuse abuse or the abandonment of guardianship support. Vale's promised savings had largely meant transferring costs to families.","The new-build hall would have to answer separate questions about its closure. Parents could steer clear of its e-commerce portal while the disputed charges were investigated. After a turbulent day, that modest freedom felt remarkably good.","As a custom, the hall kept two seats free for late arrivals who needed assistance. Elsie protected them with a vast handbag and no deference to arriving donors. The room was overcrowded again, but this time volunteers controlled the numbers before reopening another classroom. Homework help would start there tomorrow.","The repaired pool would remain closed. The caretaker explained how the wiring breakdown had caused the shutters' disruption. Damaged seals could also contaminate water; the smell had not established the nature of any emission. A proper inspection would assess harmful conditions before children returned to bathe or swim. Mara watched several parents relax at this plain answer. Nobody needed theatrical certainty to make a sensible decision.","The caretaker demonstrated the firing mechanism with its power safely isolated. Cassian watched the metal pins move and thought of the gap closing over Mara's head. She took his hand under the table, a small boost to his composure that mattered more than applause.","A school cook produced soup instead of the inedible launch cake. The new board cancelled compulsory dieting and reviewed meal intake. Children were growing people, Elsie said, rather than consumption statistics for a supplement company. Her old graduation photograph went beside information on higher education and health care. The hall seemed to grow around it.","Vale's replacement spokesperson arrived with an offer of compromise: return the disputed cash, stop the boycott and say nothing further. Mara asked whether he could put that in writing. His immediate about-face gave the lawyer a very useful minute of silence.","The proposal would not erase the false euthanasia claim against the shelter. Its vet had lost enough sleep already. Nor would avoidance restore trust in a board that had practised discrimination. Families would decide their own response; no agreement would impose censorship on them. The spokesperson left before supper, nearly tripping over the bowling ball.","Cassian watched Mara trying not to laugh. “You may fling it after him if you wish.”","“That would be a foul.”","“A subtle one.”","She gave the ball a flip towards the cupboard instead. He intercepted it badly. The resulting crash managed to rouse a child from a doze, but damaged only his reputation. The children decided to hold an entirely unofficial sports contest. Its rules covered a wide range of imaginary emergencies. Mara won the round requiring someone to repel an invading cushion. Cassian's attempt to gallop with a beanbag on his head produced havoc.","The police called Elsie with an update: statements had been received, and the money preserved pending inquiry. Speculation about imprisonment was premature. A parent who had repeated an old homicide rumour corrected it in front of those who had heard it. Mara admired that more than another loud declaration that Vale was evil.","Rain continued. There would be no hike tomorrow unless the paths became stable. The scout leader proposed indoor exploration of the history exhibition instead. Two girls had prepared questions about the memoir Industrialisation and Our Street; another had subtitled Industrialization at the River for a hard-of-hearing grandfather. Their discussion of degradation, wage devaluation and standards of living was likely to be more lively than Vale had anticipated.","The sponsor had wanted homogenization: identical classes, identical supplements, identical promises across every branch. The volunteers wanted room for local needs. Their pushback was sometimes untidy, but Mara could see the difference in a little boy patiently helping his grandmother ascend the stage. Money alone would not explain that interaction.","A driver offered to sew the torn shelter banner. He had learned while recovering from an injury. Cassian looked surprised. “I have wasted my own convalescences.”","“You spent yours flirting with me,” Mara said.","“I withdraw the remark.”","They ate soup beside the stage. The advent of this accidental festival had brought fairly modest results: safe rooms, meals, an open vote and a renewed chance to challenge unfair bills. It would not solve every problem psychologically or materially. People could still be affected negatively by the disruption; the refund process would matter after the applause ended.","Mara found that an oddly satisfying victory. Nobody had to plead with a benefactor to keep their place. Nobody had to grope through a deliberately dark corridor. Cassian offered his arm, without trying to hijack the closing dance. She took it and led him into the middle of the floor.","“This is not boring,” he said.","“Bless your standards.”","His hand settled warmly at her waist. Outside, the old centre stood dark and temporarily quiet. Inside, the music grew bold. For once, espionage consisted entirely of children spying on the married couple behind the scenery. Mara <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-4816\" data-words=\"4816\">let</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8426\" data-words=\"8426\">them</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">have</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8424\" data-words=\"8424\">their</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3475\" data-words=\"3475\">fun</a>.</p><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"100\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"102\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x003c01","title":"The Bride in Running Shoes","volume":"婚后外传：跑错路线的慈善赛","anchor":"chapter-0103","paragraphs":["Cassian arrived for the charity marathon in a shirt that made every volunteer reconsider the weather. Mara looked him over, then adjusted his number so that it covered rather less of him. Her involvement in his clothing had become a marriage ritual. Rowan called it manipulation. She called it public service.","The route crossed an old mining district, now a museum and retail street. Its renewal programme funded recreation for teenagers and scientific research into damaged land. A global corporation had offered sponsorship, minus a formidable administration fee. The museum director wanted to abolish that fee before signing. He had asked Mara to come because the sponsor's representative was pushy, and she was unsurpassed at smiling while saying no.","The band increased its volume. Cassian promised to shave before the evening dinner if Mara promised not to enter him in another race. His lapse into domestic bargaining delighted her. Their little joke restored a liveliness the sponsor's arrival had briefly killed; personal revenge could wait until the finish line.","His publishing partner awaited a ruling on access to the old trial papers. The museum would not renege on its promise to print them. A sponsor's invasion of the programme could hardly be called harmless mischief if it forced the testimony out. What Knox described as normalization looked like the suppression of an awkward story.","From the bridge they could see gulls soar above a long-fished channel. Sightseeing boats moved below, a reminder that the mining district had other sources of income. A slowdown here would affect families already living on social security. Mara hoped the day would succeed without becoming another donor's shopping spree.","A brass band played a repetitive march beside a soccer pitch. Children began to stomp along. Rowan, overdressed for running, wore a coat suitable for a prosecution. “This is a dreadful idea,” he said. “I haven't finished my tea.”","“Your performance will be our salvation,” Mara replied.","An aggressive representative named Knox approached with a contract. Its penalties could turn a fundraising spree into a net loss. He threatened a lawsuit if the museum withdrew. Cassian's easy stride stopped beside him. The contest between his calm and Knox's intimidation was so brief that Mara almost missed it.","Inside, visitors were viewing a history of nationalisation. The exhibition followed an armed raid on workers, a massacre and the subsequent reform of mine ownership. A video described rebellion; a display case held proceedings from a prosecution for sedition. Mara stopped to read a miner's prayer, copied on the back of a wage notice. The violence was historical. The temptation to use it for glamorous publicity was present.","Knox wanted a simulation of the raid in the entrance hall. The director called that contemptible. Performing a slaughter for tourist entertainment would oversimplify events and dishonour living relatives. Knox accused him of political opposition. The director's neutrality concerned present party campaigning; it did not require silence about past deaths.","The proposed simulation also included an artificial riot. A conservator had already raised the possibility of professional malpractice if fragile letters were exposed to heat. A dormant furnace stood behind the stage; nobody wanted a publicity stunt to wake it. The evening solo would use a simple lamp instead.","Mara walked with a volunteer to check the museum's lateral passage. The ventilation fan had stopped. A technological overhaul had left the controls behind a locked panel, and the repair firm would not release a code. Knox offered to hasten repairs if the director signed. It was an inexcusable use of public health concerns as a bargaining tool.","“Assuming we decline?” she asked.","“Then you wait.”","The volunteer gave a blunt sniff. The smell of hot plastic was new. Mara asked everyone to leave that wing while an electrician examined it. Fire prevention came before contractual rivalry. They could ventilate with open doors elsewhere; they could not safely keep visitors beside an unknown electrical fault.","In the clinic tent outside, a nurse was discussing family planning with adults. Her leaflet separated sex education from assumptions about procreation. Sexual health funding had been included in the sponsor's promised spending. Now Knox wanted to poach the nurse for a publicity photo while cutting her hours. Mara watched the nurse refuse. “Our service doesn't turn on whether somebody will pose for your brochure.”","“Most of these difficulties stem from that contract,” Rowan said.","“No doubt,” Cassian
